# THE MIRROR
->Or<-
## The Touchstone of Pastors:

**Wherein all preachers**
**and teachers of this final world should**
behold themselves / and according to their conscience / as before
God’s all-seeing and judging eyes / without
hypocrisy toward themselves / seriously test and examine themselves /
Whether they be true / known and sent by GOD as preachers /
teachers / bishops / and superintendents <note>In the Lutheran church, a Superintendent is a high-ranking cleric roughly equivalent to a bishop.</note> / or
not; Whether they resemble the true or false prophets;
Whether they bear the image of Christ or of the Antichrist; Whether they
are marked with the signs and characteristics
of true or false apostles.

Set forth for the godly / and those who allow
themselves to be taught and rebuked by the Spirit
of God / for Brotherly-Christian
remembrance / awakening / testing / and improvement;
but for the godless / hypocrites / stubborn / and gainsayers
set forth as a testimony / and placed before
their eyes upon their conscience /
according to the rule of the Word of God /
by

### Master Friedrich Breckling,
### Pastor at Handewitt / in
### devastated Holstein. <note>original: "verwüsteten Holstein." This refers to the region’s suffering during the Thirty Years' War and subsequent conflicts in the mid-17th century.</note>

# TO JESUS CHRIST,
## The King of all Kings, the Head of his Congregation, My Savior and Brother,

->To Him alone be the glory!<-

->And to his<-
->Stewards of Office<- <note>original: "Ampts-Verwaltern." This term refers to earthly rulers and officials, viewed here as deputies or administrators governing on behalf of God's ultimate authority.</note>

# King FREDERICK
->the Third,<-

## The Head over Denmark and Norway, etc.

# My King

<image-desc>Ornamental woodcut initial 'W' decorated with scrolling foliage and floral patterns.</image-desc>
**W**ish and pray I from Christ for such Divine understanding and wisdom—as the head possesses above the other members of the body—for the benefit and best of his political body, <note>original: "Politischen Leibes." This refers to the "Body Politic," a common 17th-century metaphor where the state is likened to a human body, with the monarch serving as the head and the subjects as the limbs.</note> so that He—

<summary>This page presents a formal dedication, bridging the spiritual and political worlds by addressing both Jesus Christ and King Frederick III of Denmark. It introduces the metaphor of the "Body Politic," framing the King's rule as a divine responsibility requiring heavenly wisdom.</summary>

<keywords>dedication, Jesus Christ, King Frederick III, Denmark, Norway, Body Politic, divine wisdom, sovereignty, stewardship</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the formal dedication to King Frederick III of Denmark, expanding on the metaphor introduced on the previous page. Having established the King as the "Head" of the body politic, Breckling now describes the physiological and spiritual duties that the head owes to its limbs, transitioning into a personal declaration of his own loyalty and oath-bound duty to the monarch.</meta>

# Dedication <note>original: "Dedicatio"</note>

...as the head takes care of its poor,
torn members; cares for
the same; sees and hears their
need and misery with its own eyes and ears;
suffers with them; and thus
rules them—as the head—
for the common benefit of the body;
indeed, proving in all things the
faithfulness and office of the head,
so that the head, together with the members,
may be raised up again through the
actual communication of the
<term>animal spirits</term> <note>original: "Bewegungs-geisterlein." Literally "little spirits of movement." In 17th-century science, it was believed that the brain generated subtle fluids or "spirits" that traveled through the nerves to animate the muscles. Breckling uses this as a metaphor for how a King’s wisdom should animate his subjects.</note>,
and may be strengthened and
preserved by God.

Most Serene King, since I
am bound to You by an oath of duty,
to prevent and avert damage
and disaster from You and Your kingdoms
in all things—as much in physical
as even more so in spiritual matters—
as a fellow-member under Your
protection; and conversely, to promote
Your benefit and advancement with all possible

--- Page 9 ---
<meta>This page continues the dedication to King Frederick III of Denmark. Having previously established his duty as a subject and a member of the "body politic," Breckling now moves from a general declaration of loyalty to a specific, prophetic claim: he asserts that the hardships facing the kingdom are not merely political issues, but divine punishments caused by the sins of the people and, most significantly, the corruption of the clergy.</meta>

<summary>Breckling addresses King Frederick III directly, claiming that the disasters befalling Denmark are divine judgments rather than political failures. He specifically blames the "godless" Lutheran clergy for the nation’s suffering, urging the King to judge the truth of this claim through the Holy Spirit.</summary>

# Dedication <note>original: "Dedicatio"</note>

...diligence to the best of my ability. Therefore, I have had to live up to that oath obediently, and have here—according to the light, talents <note>original: "Pfunde," literally "pounds." A reference to the biblical Parable of the Talents, where servants are expected to invest the spiritual gifts God gives them.</note>, and gifts shared with me by **God** for the benefit of His Church and the salvation of Your Realm—discovered and revealed the almost sole and foremost cause of all the misfortune, misery, and sorrow that has until now come upon You, Your children, and Your realms. This I have found not from <term>political science</term> <note>original: "Politica." In the 17th century, this referred to the secular study of governance and statecraft, which Breckling contrasts with divine revelation.</note>, but from **God's** Word through His Spirit.

Recognize that **God** has done all this to you for the sake of the sins and abominations of Yourself, Your subordinate authorities, and Your whole land—but especially because of Your godless preachers and teachers.

Test, read, and judge for yourself through **God's** Spirit according to **God's** Word: whether the preachers are not the sole and foremost cause of al-

# Dedication <note>original: "Dedicatio"</note>

<sig>A iiij</sig>

<meta>The catchword from the previous page was "Wer-" (Who), completing the first sentence of this page: "Who [shall] follow his King in this?"</meta>

Who would follow his King in this? O King, take note of this! I do not speak of the righteous, who lament this very thing with me and seek a Reformation <term>The 16th-century religious movement that aimed to reform the doctrines and practices of the Church; here, the author calls for a continuing moral and structural renewal.</term> alongside me. Fill the courts <note>original: "Gericht"</note> and the ministry <note>original: "Predigampt"</note> with conscientious people who are neither hypocrites nor greedy, but who possess the Spirit of the living God, as Jehoshaphat <note>A King of Judah (reigned c. 870–849 BCE) praised in the Bible for establishing a system of itinerant teachers and judges to uphold God's law.</note> did, 2 Chronicles 19, Deuteronomy 17. And cast out from your land the injustice along with the greedy officials <note>original: "Amptleuten"</note>, bailiffs <note>original: "Vögten," administrative officials or local governors</note>, and scribes <note>original: "Schreibern," meaning clerks or legal administrators</note> who have devastated your land and devoured your sheep and members <note>A metaphor for the common people as members of the body politic or the church "flock."</note>, so that God may also remove the punishment, Jeremiah 22, Wisdom of Solomon 6, Isaiah 32. Abandon the alliance and friendship with the godless and the idolatrous, so that the wrath and punishment of God does not continue against you, as it did against Asa and Jehoshaphat, 2 Chronicles 16 and 19 <note>In the Bible, both Kings Asa and Jehoshaphat were rebuked by prophets for making political alliances with ungodly foreign powers instead of relying solely on God.</note>.

<meta>catchword: Wer- (Who)</meta>

<summary>The author calls upon the King to purge his administration of corrupt officials and greedy clergy, using the biblical examples of Jehoshaphat and Asa to advocate for a state governed by spiritual integrity rather than political alliances.</summary>

# Dedication <note>original: "Dedicatio"</note>

Become another man, like Saul <note>A reference to 1 Samuel 10:6, where the prophet Samuel tells Saul he will be "turned into another man" by the Spirit of God.</note>, and rebuild your destroyed kingdoms in such a way, like David, so that God’s Word, the **Ark of the Covenant** <term>The sacred chest described in the Bible as containing the tablets of the Ten Commandments; here used to symbolize the presence of God's law and authority in the state.</term>, and the Church may be in your heart and find a dwelling and protection with you, Psalm 132, 2 Samuel 6, 7 et seq. Open the gate wide for Christ, my King and brother, who has knocked for so long at your door with warnings and punishments, Psalm 24. He still wishes to enter and dwell with you, and to celebrate the **Supper** <note>original: "Abendmahl"; while this usually means the Eucharist, in this context it refers to the "Supper of the Lamb" or intimate communion with God at the end of time.</note> with you in the evening of this world, and you with Him, Revelation 3 <note>original: "Apoc. 3"; refers to the biblical book of Revelation 3:20: "Behold, I stand at the door and knock."</note>. Rebuild for Him His kingdom—which has been so greatly devastated by **Satan** and his servants that He can hardly find faith and love within it anymore—and in return, He will rebuild your house and restore and renew your destroyed kingdoms for you, 2 Samuel 7. Remove all causes of this punishment as much as possible

out of your land; and remove the transgressors, slanderers, the proud, the false, liars, evildoers, hypocrites, and the godless, both in the **spiritual** and **worldly estates** <term>Refers to the "Two Estates" of early modern society: the clergy (spiritual) and the secular government or nobility (worldly).</term>; yes, root them out and destroy them, as David did in Psalm 101 <note>In Psalm 101, King David declares his intent to silence slanderers and destroy the wicked of the land to protect the city of the Lord.</note>, according to God’s Word. of God you do not follow / Your kingdom / as a **steward** <term>Amptman: A high-ranking official or deputy acting on behalf of a sovereign; here, the King is described as God's deputy on Earth.</term> of God on earth / do not restore with judgment and righteousness / and according to the order prescribed for you in His Word / do not protect, care for, provide for, and rule as a head does its members; but instead oppress your subjects / like **Pharaoh** <note>The biblical King of Egypt who enslaved the Israelites, frequently used in this period as the ultimate example of a cruel tyrant.</note> / or allow them to be further oppressed by the **taskmasters** <term>Fronvögten: Overseers or bailiffs who managed forced labor and collected dues from the peasantry.</term> / and allow your sheep to be further flayed, devoured, and torn apart by the **officials** / in that they seize everything for themselves / and, amidst the ruin of your entire land, become so rich, fat, and sleek; and if you allow your own members <note>Likening the subjects of a kingdom to the limbs of a body, of which the King is the head.</note> to be so plagued, **executed** <note>original: "exequiren," meaning to carry out a sentence or perform an execution.</note>, martyred, and tortured by those who should rather heal and care for them: if all malice and abomination prevail at your court and in all estates / and hypocrisy and injustice—

# Dedication.

<meta>catchword: VOR. (likely an abbreviation for "Vorrede" or Preface, indicating the start of the next section)</meta>

Psalm 2. Jeremiah 25. Revelation 19. So let yourself
now be instructed, disciplined, and saved
by your God, and by His
servants sent to you. Now, I have
performed my office of watchman <term>Wächter ampt: the biblical duty of a spiritual sentinel (derived from Ezekiel) tasked with warning a city or its leader of impending divine judgment</term> in the
name of the Lord, and
have warned and awakened you
against destruction and the
sword, Ezekiel 33. Heaven and
earth shall be my witness; choose

am henceforth innocent of your
destruction and blood. Now, O
King, read, test, and judge this
writing yourself, and recognize that it
comes not from a hypocrite, but
from the hand of your faithful
and obedient servant, who
loves you as a Christian and
seeks your welfare <note>original: "Wolfahrt," meaning the King's spiritual and physical well-being and the prosperity of his realm.</note> alongside
Jeremiah and Micah <note>Breckling compares himself to these Old Testament prophets who were often persecuted for telling uncomfortable truths to kings for the sake of the nation's survival.</note>,

->Master Friedrich Breckling<-

originates. Dated Flensburg,

# Preface

To keep the heart and eye in His light and power following Luther’s Reformation <note>original: "Reformation Lutheri"</note>, as well as to heal them again later—since they had been blinded by Satan (2 Corinthians 4), poisoned, and had almost died—[these men] first attacked the problem gently, and then with increasing strength and severity. This is demonstrated by the writings of Luther, Musculus <note>Wolfgang Musculus (1497–1563), a Reformer who worked in Augsburg and Bern</note>, Sarcerius <note>Erasmus Sarcerius (1501–1559), a Lutheran theologian known for his work on discipline</note>, Arndt <note>Johann Arndt (1555–1621), whose "True Christianity" focused on inner transformation</note>, Meyfart <note>Johann Matthäus Meyfart (1590–1642), a critic of both church and legal abuses</note>, Mengering <note>Arnold Mengering, a superintendent known for his "Sorrowful Penitential Shirt"</note>, Saubert <note>Johannes Saubert (1592–1646), a prominent preacher in Nuremberg</note>, Schmidt <note>Likely referring to a contemporary Lutheran scholastic or polemicist</note>, Johann Valentin Andreae <note>A theologian and social reformer famous for his vision of a "Christian Union"</note>, Breler, Betkius <note>Joachim Betke (1601–1663), a vocal critic of dead orthodoxy</note>, Egard <note>Paul Egard, a mystical-leaning Lutheran author</note>, and many hundreds of others, whose writings are as spiritual as they are sharp and earnest. 

But because such men of God achieved very little or nothing with such godly and sharp writings and warnings—so much so that things have become worse and worse over time—many people, out of mad and erroneous confusion, believe they are not blind, sick, or dead (Revelation 3) <note>original: "Apoc. 3"</note>, but rather exceedingly healthy. They have even despised such physicians of God and their writings, casting them to the wind, if not outright branding them as heretical and confiscating them. Therefore, God the Supreme Judge and Physician <note>original: "Medicus"</note> has now had to look into the matter Himself, over

### To the Reader.

[He will] keep His word and cure the heart, eye, along with the whole body in an entirely different way (Isaiah 28). Like a physician <note>original: "Medicus"</note> who takes up the extreme treatment <note>original: "extremam curam"</note> of cutting and burning, so that the healthy part is not corrupted <note>original: "ne pars sincera trahatur," literally: "lest the healthy part be dragged down"</note>, or even like an executioner <note>original: "Scharffrichter"</note> gouging out eyes, tearing out hearts, and chopping off heads (Isaiah 9), until He finally makes an end of such people—as He did with the Jews and their shepherds (Zechariah 10 and 11; Ezekiel 13, 22, and 34). This is because no fatherly warning or discipline will help them anymore (Ezekiel 21).

Since God Himself has now undertaken His cure with such a sharp hand upon us and many others, and will continue from one end of the earth to the other according to the judgment He has so long threatened against us (Jeremiah 25), we must also change our method of curing <note>original: "modum curandi"</note>. Guided by God’s hand and according to God’s Word and His preaching through deeds <note>original: "Real-Predigt"; a term referring to God speaking through historical events and physical realities rather than just scripture</note>, we must set aside our own preferences—as is necessary in desperate illnesses—lest we preach things different from God, but rather the same things as He and the Prophets <note>original: "ne diversa à Deo, sed eadem cum ipso ac Prophetis prædicemus"</note>. Therefore, we present to you here an earnest, sharp

# In the Name of Jesus /

There shortly follow the theses <note>original: "Theses"; these are propositions or points for structured debate or reflection</note>, according to which every person—alongside the Word of God—can test, judge, and examine himself as to whether he has lived until now from God or from Satan; with Christ or against Christ; for God's glory or for his own honor and benefit. Let each person’s conscience—and for me in Him, yes, even against Him himself—form the hypothesis <note>the specific premise or "case study" of one's own life</note>, categorize <term>subsumiren; a logical term meaning to bring a specific instance under a general rule</term>, and conclude. This is for whoever is willing to let himself be convinced, judged, and condemned by the Spirit of God and his own conscience for his own salvation, so that God may make him blessed again.

### VI. Concerning the Method of Teaching. <note>original: *A Methodo docendi*</note>

Those who do not teach thoroughly and orderly, like **Christ** and the wise master builder **Paul**, in such a way that they first lead people to the knowledge of themselves—of their own misery, the unspeakable corruption of their entire nature with all its powers, senses, and endeavors, through **original sin** <term>Erbsünde: the theological concept that humanity inherited a fallen, sinful nature from Adam</term> and the **Fall of Adam**, and the temporal and eternal punishments, death, and damnation that followed—so that they thus reach a **salutary despair** <note>A "healthy" or "saving" despair where one realizes they cannot save themselves, which is the necessary precursor to seeking God's grace.</note> of themselves, and a loss of heart in all their own abilities, powers, goodness, wisdom, and righteousness;

so that they are led away from this false foundation, built upon **Christ** the right **cornerstone**, and—remaining immovable against all the gates of hell—are founded through the **righteousness of faith** which alone counts before God, and are placed into the perfect freedom of faith, eternal joy of conscience, and unwavering certainty of their **salvation**: consequently having their hearts changed by the Spirit of God, being united with Christ, and made alive through Him

<meta>catchword: and</meta>

Concerning free will and its **impossibility** of achieving its own renewal and conversion from all that into which we have fallen through Adam’s Fall: Concerning the Law, its manner, nature, properties, and twofold office <note>In Lutheran theology, the "twofold office" or "uses" of the Law typically refer to its power to maintain civil order and, more importantly, to reveal human sinfulness to lead one to repentance.</note>—namely, to reveal sin and wrath, or the punishment for sins—so that it may drive us away from ourselves and toward Christ; concerning the so highly necessary distinction between the Law and the Gospel <note>original: *Gesezes und Evangelii*</note>, and between the Old and New Testaments, which was so strongly commended to us by the late Luther <note>original: *Luthero Seel.*—the abbreviation 'Seel.' stands for *selig*, meaning "of blessed memory," used for the deceased</note>, and upon which almost everything depends: Concerning the abrogation <term>Abrogation: the abolition or repeal of a law; here referring to the belief that Christians are no longer under the condemnation of the Old Testament law</term> of the Law, including the Moral Law, among believers, who have died to it through the body of Christ, so that they may henceforth serve **God** in the new essence of the Spirit, and should not be under the Law, but under Grace.

## VIII. From Life and Example. <note>original Latin: "A Vita & Exemplo."</note>

Those who do not adorn their teaching in all respects with a godly life; who do not first teach and discipline themselves before they teach others; who do not lead a divine and blameless life and conduct in this perverse and degenerate world, like lights in the darkness (Philippians 2, Matthew 5); who do not, like Paul, discipline <note>The original German "betäuben" implies subduing or buffeting the body to bring it under control.</note> their bodies, etc. (1 Corinthians 9); who do not prevent and avoid even the appearance of evil, so that no one may speak ill of them; who are not holy, righteous, without greed, and blameless among their listeners (1 Thessalonians 2); who do not follow Christ in His life, so that they truly deny themselves with Him, humble themselves, the...

Do they not rather hate them, allowing them to fall into the hands of the executioner? So it is also in the Church of God.

<meta>This page continues the argument from page 239, where the author draws parallels between historical "false teachers" and contemporary figures. It moves from listing biblical archetypes to applying these judgments to the current era, citing the works of the Reformation and 17th-century social critics.</meta>

<page-num>240.</page-num>

At the same time, everything that God has punished in all false prophets, antichrists, and apostles since the beginning of the world should also be applied to these individuals. They must be tested and examined against the Word of God according to all their traits and signs, so that the whole world may recognize whose servants they are—whether of Christ or of Satan—provided they do not repent. This is just as the "great Antichrist" <note>In the context of the Reformation, this term was almost exclusively used by Protestants to refer to the Papacy.</note> was already dissected <note>original: "anatomiret," meaning a systematic or "anatomical" exposure of faults.</note> and revealed by Luther 130 years ago.

Likewise, these "little antichrists" (1 John 2) <note>Referring to 1 John 2:18: "even now many antichrists have come."</note>—who in this final hour sit once again in the temple of God and exalt themselves above all else (2 Thessalonians 2)—shall have their folly made manifest according to the testimony of the Spirit of God (2 Timothy 3). Indeed, they bear the image of the "great one" in almost every detail. This process of revelation has already been started by Johann Valentin Andreae <note>A prominent German theologian and social reformer (1586–1654), famous for his satirical and mystical writings.</note> in his *APAP* <note>Refers to his work *Admonitio ad Christianos* (Warning to Christians).</note>, *Mythology*, *Menippus*, and his other "golden writings"; by Brellerus <note>Jacob Breller, a theologian and polemicist of the era.</note> in his *Mystery of Iniquity* <note>original: "Mysterio iniquitatis."</note> and *Defense for the Mystery* <note>original: "vindiciis pro Mysterio."</note>; and by many others. Furthermore, all of Luther’s writings against the great Antichrist, in certain—

By what means and by what standard every clergyman may meanwhile recognize, compare, judge in all matters, test, and avoid them according to God’s Word, just as one avoids wolves, lions, and basilisks.

## XX. By Comparison with the Prophets. <note>original Latin: "A Collatione eorum cum Prophetis."</note>

Whoever has not, for many years now, preached to this present, abominable world—which in all its social ranks is more over-confident <note>The original German uses the word *sicher* (secure/certain), which in this period implies a dangerous spiritual complacency or a false sense of safety from divine judgment despite living in sin.</note> than in all times past—revealing to it the abominations and sins inherited from its fathers, and thereupon announcing the judgment of God, indeed all temporal, spiritual, and eternal punishments, just as they are described in the Prophets and were threatened by them to their people so long ago, even under pious kings like Uzziah, Hezekiah, Josiah, etc.; indeed, God still preaches that same firm prophetic Word over us today in reality <note>original: "realiter"</note> and fulfills it daily in deed: Whoever does not still today preach in harmony with God, and to this world and "Egypt" <note>Egypt is used here as a biblical metaphor for a state of spiritual bondage, stubbornness, or worldliness.</note>—which is more hardened and over-confident amidst its punishments than ever before—does not preach that which, namely

<meta>This page continues the author’s sharp critique of the religious establishment, moving from a general condemnation of "abominations" to a specific psychological and spiritual profile of those he calls "fanatics." <page-num>262.</page-num>

I only ask this of these **fanatics** <note>original: "Phantasten." In the 17th century, this was a derogatory term for individuals who followed their own "phantasms" or imaginations rather than what the writer considered sound, objective truth.</note>, **enthusiasts** <note>original: "Enthusiasten." This term was often used by the religious establishment to mock those who claimed to be directly inspired by the Holy Spirit.</note>, and madmen, who through the enchantment of Satan are so certain of their faith, life, Christianity, office, word, and worship (which are yet pure **illusions** <note>original: *Phantasmata*</note>, dreams, and **seizures by Satan** <note>original: *raptus Satanæ*</note>) that they even believe they are the very best servants of Christ in the world. They even dare to present to Christ, the all-knowing judge, such "faithful services" they performed for him against Satan, in the hope of being preferred above all others for that reason: Matthew 7, **"Lord, have we not [prophesied] in your name?" etc.** <note>original: *Domine, nonne in nomine tuo? &c.*</note> Indeed, they even kill Christ, Jeremiah, and all those sent by God as "deceivers," acting out of a supposed great concern for the "poor deceived people" (John 11). They do this with the intention of serving God in the best possible way (John 16) and to reconcile the land with God through such an **offscouring** <note>original: *Feg-opfer*. Drawing on 1 Corinthians 4:13, this refers to someone treated as the refuse or "scum" of the earth, whose removal is thought to cleanse or appease God on behalf of the community.</note> (1 Corinthians 4).

I ask this: if someone—three or four, or even twenty, thirty, or a hundred years ago (as **Luther** once did sufficiently in Germany)—had continuously preached this to us in all stations of life, right to our faces, and had applied the prophets to us so **plainly** <note>original: "Teutsch" (Germanly). Used idiomatically here to mean "clearly" or "in plain, unmistakable language."</note> as it...

<summary>The author describes the "spiritual delusion" of religious persecutors who believe their violence and rigid adherence to ritual are godly services. Page 231. <note>This refers to the page number in Paul Egard’s book *Mundo immundo* (The Impure World) from which these notes are being taken.</note> If one excludes a few / who truly mean Christ / then all the others are of one kind and spirit.

There are few divines <note>German: *Gottsgelehrten*, literally "those learned in God," referring to spiritually enlightened theologians.</note> / but of world-scholars <note>German: *Welt-gelehrten*, referring to those with academic knowledge but no spiritual depth.</note> there is a great multitude. The common crowd of teachers does not know Jesus / whom they nonetheless preach and have in their mouths; they know him only according to the letter / but not according to the spirit and power / they do not know Christ’s cross and life: They are blind: Their theology <note>original Latin: *Theologia*.</note> is sheer darkness.

Is it a wonder / that many people go astray / when the shepherd himself is blind? Matthew 15. The Bible is a sealed book to them: Where they should know nothing / there they know everything; and where they should know everything / there they know nothing: Their theology is not written in their hearts and united with them / but only in their books.

Ah, how many blind watchmen and silent dogs <note>A common polemical reference to Isaiah 56:10, describing leaders who fail to warn their people of spiritual danger.</note> the Lord Jesus has! They punish out of fleshly zeal / see page 66. <note>original Latin: *vid. pag. 66*.</note>

# Luther’s Prophecy, which he
## Written in the Year 1546, shortly before his death.

UNFORTUNATELY, the ingratitude of the world is so exceedingly great—and grows larger day by day—that if the Last Judgment <note>The final judgment of humanity at the end of time</note> did not intervene, the days would have to be shortened with sorrow. Indeed, it is not just with sorrow, but we must certainly prophesy and expect the abominable and terrible plague and wrath of God, by which He will withdraw His Light back to Himself and allow darkness to fall over everything. Such a plague (unfortunately) already affects the majority, because almost the entire crowd has lost God’s Word in their hearts and despises it so miserably.

On the other hand, they have clung to the idol Mammon <term>Mammon: wealth or material greed, personified as a false god in the New Testament</term> with such diligence and chased after him constantly, as if every person wanted to seize all the world’s goods for themselves. One sees this daily: how the beloved Word still shines only a little from the pulpit through the physical voice, though even those pulpits are now few in number. 

Since we then see and grasp how the divine Word is already extinguished in people's hearts, it will take very little for it to be extinguished on the pulpit as well; for he who has no heart for it will not long lend his ears or mouth to it. But when the pulpit no longer shines, then the world has

365.
R iij

Luther: in the second sermon <note>original: *Conc:* for *Concio*</note> for the first Sunday of Advent.
Now the people are possessed by seven devils, whereas they were previously possessed by only one devil: The devil now drives into the people in droves; for now, under the bright light of the Gospel, they are greedier, more cunning, more self-seeking, more unmerciful, more insolent, and worse than they were before under the Papacy <note>original: *Papstumb*</note>, etc. This is seen among peasants, citizens, nobility, and all estates, from the highest to the lowest—what a shameful, disorderly life they lead in greed, drinking, gluttony, unchastity, and all sorts of shame and vice.

---

In the House Postils <note>original: *Postilla domestica*, a collection of Martin Luther's sermons intended for family use at home</note>, leaf 373. If the Last Day <note>Judgment Day</note> does not come soon, it will become just as bad again—yes, worse than under the Papacy—so that even the doctors <note>meaning high-ranking theological scholars</note> and teachers will know nothing of God and His commandments, and they will know even less of Christ.

---

Volume 4, German Edition, leaf 248. I find daily that there are very few preachers now who can correctly and well understand and teach the Ten Commandments, the Creed <note>original: *Glauben*</note>, and the Lord’s Prayer for the poor people.

366.

The Gospel <note>original Latin: *Evangelium*</note> is handled in such a way that it differs in no way from laws and moral precepts: the knowledge of Faith and grace is held in contempt even by the theologians themselves.

---

Volume 2, leaf 255. The world is full of the lips, tongues, and voices of preachers, but nowhere is the succulent and nimble tongue of Christ to be found.

---

Volume 2, leaf 379. Those who now teach the Gospel best make our Christ out to be a schoolmaster or a servant who stays on the outside teaching good things, rather than one who rules within, performing good works. <note>Luther is criticizing a view of Christ as merely a moral example rather than a living savior who transforms the believer from the inside.</note>

---

Volume 4, leaf 344. Our word—or rather, God's—is despised by our very own people <note>original Latin: *nostrissimis*, literally "those most our own," referring to fellow reformers or followers</note>.

---

Volume 6, leaf 215. I say it upon my soul, as much as I have seen and experienced: both preachers and writers, who now want and ought to be the best—with very few exceptions—know absolutely nothing about this matter (namely, the article of Justification <term>Justification: The central Reformation doctrine that a person is declared righteous by God through faith alone, rather than through their own deeds or "works"</term>), and even if they sometimes happen to guess correctly, it is spoken or heard as if in a dream.

---

Volume 4, leaf 100. The true and proper use of the law will be obscured again after our times and will be utterly overwhelmed. For while we are still living and exercising with the greatest diligence the office <note>Luther is discussing the "office" or duty of distinguishing between the Law (which commands) and the Gospel (which promises)</note> of both Law and Gospel

...demonstrating its office and Use, there are very few even among those who wish to seem pious and profess the Gospel <note>original: *Evangelium*</note> with us, who hold these things correctly. What do you think will happen once we are gone?

---

Thus, leaf 130. Many will arise wanting to be Masters <note>meaning teachers or authoritative leaders</note>, who under the pretext of piety will teach perverse things, and in a short time will subvert everything that we have built over a long time and with great effort.

Volume 1, German, leaf 556. The greedy and the ambitious <note>original German: *Die Geitzigen und Ehrgeitzigen*</note> should not be preachers of the Gospel; instead, it must be done by those who care for no possessions, honor, pleasure, nor even life.

---

In summary: The time predicted long ago is here—that after the Antichrist <note>Luther commonly identified the institutional Papacy of his time as the "Antichrist" because he believed it sat in God's temple while subverting the Gospel</note> was revealed, there would be people who live without God, each following their own desires and delusions. For just as the Popes set themselves above God, now everyone wants to act without God; and since they are free from the laws of the Pope, they want to be free from the laws of God as well, following nothing but politics <term>Politics: From the Latin *Politica*, referring here to worldly administration or secular governance as a replacement for spiritual or divine guidance</term>. Yet while we imagine those people are far away, it is we ourselves who are doing such things.

Who does not see and grasp this, unless he is [blinder] than a mole... <note>The phrase ends mid-sentence. The original text uses the Latin *Talpa* (mole), and the catchword for the next page completes the idiom.</note>

358.

...is blinder <note>original Latin: *cœcior*</note> [than a mole], seeing how these and other prophecies of Luther have in fact already long been fulfilled among us today; and that what he writes in Volume 1 <term>Volume: From the Latin *Tomus* (abbreviated as *Tom.*), referring to one book in a multi-volume collection of writings</term> of the German works, leaf 94, is still true of many of us:

> The learned are the perverted; the wise, the unwise; the rational, the irrational. The more highly learned, the lower the pupil; the richer the reason, the poorer the understanding; indeed, the more violent an enemy and persecutor of Christ. 1 Corinthians 2.

Volume 4, Latin, on Genesis <note>original: *in Genes.*</note>. Nowadays, many speak of the Gospel as if they were angels, but when you look at their lives and works, you find nothing but devils. For the devil does not sleep; he will eventually bring it to pass that no proper pulpit <note>original German: *Predigstul*</note> can be found in all of Germany, says Luther once again. Whether this has not indeed occurred, I leave to the clergy to judge.

Luther, Volume 2, Jena Edition <note>The Jena edition was a major 16th-century collection of Luther's works, often favored by those who held to a stricter interpretation of his teachings</note>, leaf 187. If I am to have any fault, I would rather speak too harshly and thrust out the truth too bluntly <note>original German: *unvernünfftig*, literally "unreasonably" or "without worldly reason"</note> than to ever play the hypocrite or keep the truth to myself.

<summary>This page cites various volumes of Luther's works to argue that his "prophecies" about the decline of the German church and the arrogance of the learned have come true. It emphasizes Luther's preference for harsh truth over hypocritical silence.</summary>

369.

Volume 2, in the Funeral Sermon <note>original Latin: *Conc. Funebr.*</note> for Duke Frederick: I fear that in time it will come to this: when one wants to punish <note>The German *straffen* implies both a verbal rebuke and a moral correction</note> vices, even those who wish to be Christians will take offense at it, and will interpret such necessary punishment as if their honor were being slandered.

Volume 5, German edition, leaf 122: No sin offends and displeases God more than when one wants to deny, gloss over, and hide public wickedness.

In the Church Postil <term>Church Postil: A *Postilla* was a collection of sermons on the Bible readings assigned for each Sunday of the church year, widely used for teaching and devotion</term> on the Epistle for the 5th Sunday after Trinity: "Indeed, you are nonetheless attacking my office <term>Office: From the German *Ampt*, referring to the divinely appointed duty or position of a pastor</term> in which I have been placed." Response: "No, dear brother, it is not called 'attacking the office' when someone tells you and me that we are not acting rightly in our office, or that we are not conducting it as we should. Rather, the Word of God punishes us for precisely this reason: that we bring dishonor upon the Divine Office and thus misuse it against His command. Therefore, you must not call me to account for this," etc.

Look for such and a thousand other testimonies concerning false teachers in Luther yourself, until we, in due time, gather them from him and [expose] their folly

<summary>This page records Martin Luther's warnings against the hypocrisy of those who refuse to accept moral rebuke and his defense of using the Word of God to correct those in positions of church authority.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion from the previous page, concluding the thought regarding gathering testimonies from Martin Luther’s works to expose the folly of false teachers. 370.

...[be able to] reveal out of and with Luther. Meanwhile, see M. Statius's *Lutherus Redivivus* <note>Luther Revived</note> and *Cynosura* <note>The Guiding Star</note>: likewise the *Book of Luther’s Prophecies*.

Whoever desires more testimonies <note>original Latin: *testimonia*</note> should read—alongside the authors cited above—the writings <note>original Latin: *scripta*</note> of Johann Valentin Andreae; Dr. Schmidt, President in Strasbourg; Johann Meyfart; Paul Egard; Johann Arndt; Joachim Betke; Christian Hohburg; Johann Saubert; Mengering; Ericus Rhoneus’s *Concerning the Office of Rebuke of the Spirit*; Ernestus Wesichius’s *Touchstone of the Saints*; and other still-upright Lutheran theologians who, along with me, lament the suffering of Joseph <term>Suffering of Joseph: A reference to Amos 6:6, used by these authors to describe the spiritual decay and "brokenness" of the church</term>; but read especially Luther, who alone is worth them all <note>original Latin: *qui unus instar omnium*</note>.

> General rebukes should be harmful to no one, but are lawful and permitted by every right. However, if anyone takes what is said in general to themselves and thinks it is directed at them, they betray themselves by their own evidence, like a shrew-mouse <note>The Latin *sorex* (shrew) refers to an old proverb about a creature that betrays its presence by its own noise</note>, and by this very act indicate their own guilty conscience.

Jerome <note>Hieronymus</note> in the *Epistle to Nepotian*: We have harmed no one; no person’s name has been singled out in my writing or speech; our discourse has struck no one specifically; [it was] general...

<summary>The author points the reader toward a collection of Lutheran and proto-Pietist theologians who critiqued the church's corruption, concluding with a Latin defense of using general rebukes to address widespread moral failings.</summary>
<keywords>Martin Luther, Martinus Statius, Johann Arndt, Johann Valentin Andreae, corruption, church reform, rebukes, Jerome, suffering of Joseph, Lutheran theology</keywords>

--- Page 311 ---
<meta>This page continues the excerpt from Saint Jerome's "Epistle to Nepotian" from the previous page, where he argues that general critiques of vice should not be taken as personal attacks unless the reader has a guilty conscience. It then moves into further testimonies from Jan Hus, Caspar Barlaeus, and Erasmus on the same theme: the defensiveness of corrupt clergy.</meta>

371.

...the discussion was about vices, but whoever wishes to be angry with me confesses of himself that he is such a person <note>original Latin: *quod talis sit confitetur*</note>.

*Jan Hus in the book "On the Abominations of Carnal Priests," chapter 67.*
This one notable and characteristic trait accompanies carnal clerics, and especially the Priests <term>Sacerdotes: specifically referencing those in the priesthood who lead worldly rather than spiritual lives</term>: that they detest correction, etc. The word of correction finds no progress in them, etc. They are more ready to be the first to attack and persecute faithful writers and those who rebuke crimes, etc.

*Barlaeus.* <note>Caspar Barlaeus (1584–1648), a Dutch polymath and theologian</note>
They think everything said against them is too much, but what they themselves say against others is [considered] less than what was owed; they want others to be lenient toward them, while they themselves are most rigid toward others, etc. Moreover, they never rage more than when they think their order <note>meaning their social rank or clerical class</note> has been offended, etc. Here they issue a decree <note>original: *Senatus-Consultum*, a formal Roman-style legislative order</note> about taking revenge on the rebuker, whom they denounce with one voice as an enemy of the Church, a fighter against Christ <note>original: *Christomachum*</note>, and a slanderer of the pious servants of God, etc.; and they fly at him like hornets, and sting wherever they can, etc.

*Erasmus in the defense of Folly.* <note>Referring to Erasmus’s defense of his famous work "The Praise of Folly" (Moriae Encomium)</note>
Those who take offense there where no person’s name is published seem to me to be not far removed from the emotions of silly women, who, if anything is said against bad women, are so moved as if that insult pertained to each one of them individually. Let this kind of foolishness be far from theologians! If I encounter here [in the writing] some crime from which I am immune, I am not offended, but I congratulate myself that I am free from those evils to which I see many are subject. But if some sore point <note>original: *hulcus*, literally an ulcer or wound</note> has been touched, and I have been shown to myself in a mirror, then there is no reason why...

<meta>catchword: [be] offended (offendi)</meta>

<summary>This page compiles quotes from Jan Hus, Caspar Barlaeus, and Erasmus to argue that corrupt clergy reveal their own guilt when they react aggressively to general criticisms of church vices. The authors compare such defensive behavior to "hornets" or "silly women" who take collective insults personally.</summary>

<keywords>Jan Hus, Barlaeus, Erasmus, carnal priests, clerical corruption, defensiveness, The Praise of Folly, rebuking sin</keywords>

--- Page 312 ---
<meta>This page concludes the excerpt from Desiderius Erasmus’s "The Praise of Folly" (Moriae Encomium), where he defends his satire against the thin-skinned reactions of the clergy. This completes the body of the manuscript before transitioning to the printing house's list of corrections (Errata).</meta>

372.

...should I be offended, etc. Rather, having been warned, I will take care that such a reproach cannot be cast specifically in my face hereafter, which I see noted there without any name being mentioned, etc. I ask you, do you think the order of Theologians <note>original: *Theologorum ordinem*</note> is provoked if something is said against foolish or bad Theologians who are unworthy of that name, etc.? For this matter does not pertain to an insult of the order, but to the praise of those few who have conducted themselves most beautifully in that most beautiful order. What is said against the bad does not apply to the good. Let us rejoice rather that the good are warned not to degenerate into the bad, and the bad are warned to cease being so, etc. If anyone stands forth and cries out that he is injured, he betrays either his conscience <note>original: *conscientiam*</note> or certainly his fear. It is not the class of men itself that is being noted, but those who are the bad ones within a good class.

->From Jesus to Jesus. Alpha and Omega. To God alone be the glory.<-

Page 40, line 1: for *Sincerity* <note>original spelling: *Hertzligkeit*</note> / read *Sincerity* <note>corrected: *Hertzlichkeit*</note>. Page 42, line 16: for *Plans* / read *in Plans*. Same page, line 26: for *false* / read *the False*. Page 59, line 27: for *to* / read *to the*. Page 60, line 5: for *convert* / read *pervert*. Same page, line 18: for *Teaching* / read *Teachers*. Page 61, line 10: for *we* / read *whoever*. Page 64, line 21: for *attain* / read *incite*. Page 70, line 3: for *of this* / read *of those*. Page 78, line 12: for *to* / read *to the*. Same page, line 21: for *to show God* / read *to show oneself to God*. Same page, line 16: for *lamentably* / read *daily*. Page 84, line 5: for *draw* / read *adorn*. The tone shifts significantly from Erasmus’s scholarly satire to Breckling’s fiery, apocalyptic polemic against the religious and political establishment.</meta>

### In the Name of Jesus.

# Proclamation of the
## Day of Vengeance and Judgment
# of GOD/

To the godless authorities of today/
Kings/ Princes/ Priests/ Scribes <note>referring to theologians and legal scholars</note>/
Subjects/ Listeners and all false
Christians in all stations/ sects and
places/ who have fallen away from their head, Christ/
abandoned the fellowship with him/ and the brotherly love,
loyalty, and unity among one another in their hearts/ and 
have entered into the fellowship of the world under their head, the
Devil, against Christ/ whereby the unity
in Spirit is entirely severed/ the bond of love and
peace is completely dissolved/ all kinds of discord/ war and
confusion have been wrought upon God’s
earth/ and the whole body finally
must perish.

->By<-

## FRIEDRICH BRECKLING

->Servant of Christ, the Supreme King and Judge over all kings.<-

> 2 Cor. 5. We are ambassadors in Christ’s stead <note>original: *Botschafften an Christus statt*</note>/ James
> 5. Behold, the judge stands before the door/ Isaiah 63. The day
> of vengeance/ the year to redeem his own has come/
> Revelation 14. Fear God and give him the glory/ for
> the time of his judgment has come.

---

->Amsterdam/ printed by Christoffel Conradus,
for the Author, in the year 1660.<-
